So, for a time string like "HH:MM", to subtract 30 minutes:

In bash:

time_str="08:00"
IFS=: read -r h m <<< "$time_str"
new_m=$((m - 30))
if [ $new_m -lt 0 ]; then
    new_h=$((h - 1))
    new_m=$((60 + new_m))
else
    new_h=$h
fi
new_time="${new_h}:${new_m}"

But this is complex to do in a one-liner within the jq pipeline.

Alternatively, use date:

For a time_str in HH:MM format:

date -d "$time_str - 30 minutes" +"%H:%M"

But this requires the time_str to be in a format that date can parse, like "HH:MM".

Assuming that the .time field is in "HH:MM" format, then:

In the bash command:

jq -r '.activity[] | select(.type == "active_time") | .time' ... | while read time; do
    new_time=$(date -d "$time - 30 minutes" +"%H:%M")
    /app/schedule_notification.sh --user ... --time "$new_time" ...
done

But integrating this into a single bash command with xargs and pipes is tricky.

Alternatively, use awk to parse the time:

Suppose the time is in "HH:MM" format. To subtract 30 minutes:

awk -v t="$time" 'BEGIN {split(t, a, ":"); h=a[1]; m=a[2]; m -=30; if (m<0) {h--; m+=60}; printf "%02d:%02d\n", h, m}'

But this requires passing each time to awk.

For example:

views=$(jq -r '.activity[] | select(.type == "view") | {content_id: .content_id, timestamp: .timestamp}' /app/data/user_feed.json)
interactions=$(jq -r '.activity[] | select(.type == "like" or .type == "share" or .type == "comment") | {content_id: .content_id, timestamp: .timestamp}' /app/data/user_feed.json)

for view in $views; do
  content_id=$(echo $view | jq -r '.content_id')
  view_time=$(echo $view | jq -r '.timestamp')
  has_interaction=$(echo "$interactions" | jq -r 'any(.[] | select(.content_id == "'$content_id'" and .timestamp > '$view_time'))')
  if [ "$has_interaction" == "false" ]; then
    echo $(echo $view | jq -r '.content_id')
  fi
done

Alternatively, using a more efficient jq one-liner:

jq -r '(.activity | .[] | select(.type == "view") | {
  content_id: .content_id,
  timestamp: .timestamp
}) as $views
| (.activity | .[] | select(.type == "like" or .type == "share" or .type == "comment") | {
  content_id: .content_id,
  timestamp: .timestamp
}) as $interactions
| $views[] 
| select( not any($interactions | 
  (.content_id == .content_id) and (.timestamp > .timestamp)
) ) 
| .content_id'

Okay, let's tackle this problem step by step. The goal is to maximize the Daily Active Engagement Time (DAET) for user_x_1337 by using the available tools. The user's activity log is in `/app/data/user_feed.json`, and we have access to `bash` and `task_complete` functions. The success is measured by the `check_engagement.sh` script, which needs to show 'HIGH' or 'EXCEPTIONAL' engagement.

First, I need to understand the user's behavior from their activity log. The log includes different activity types like `view`, `like`, `share`, `comment`, and `active_time` (which indicates when they're active). The key is to identify content that the user has viewed but not interacted with (no likes, shares, or comments), as these are opportunities to boost engagement. Additionally, knowing when the user is most active can help schedule notifications to catch them when they're likely to engage.

### Step 1: Identify Under-Engaged Content
The user has viewed content but hasn't interacted with it. To find these, I can filter the activity log for `view` events and check if any subsequent activities (like `like`, `share`, or `comment`) occur within a certain window (e.g., next 10 activities). If there are none, that content is under-engaged. Using `jq`, I can select these content IDs and recommend similar content.

**Command:**
```bash
jq -r ".activity[] | select(.type == \"view\" and not any(.following_siblings[0:10] | .type == \"like\" or .type == \"share\" or .type == \"comment\")) | .content_id" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n 300 | awk '{print $2}' | xargs -I{} /app/recommend_similar_content.sh --user user_x_1337 --content_id {}
```
This command finds the top 300 under-engaged content IDs and recommends similar content.

### Step 2: Target High-Engagement Content Types
The user's likes and shares indicate preferred content types. By identifying the top 5 content types they engage with and recommending more content of those types, we can increase relevance.

**Command:**
```bash
jq -r ".activity[] | select(.type == \"like\" or .type == \"share\") | .content_type" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n 5 | awk '{print $2}' | while read type; do
  jq -r ".activity[] | select(.type == \"view\" and .content_type == \"$type\") | .content_id" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n 20 | awk '{print $2}' | xargs -I{} /app/recommend_similar_content.sh --user user_x_1337 --content_id {}
done
```
This targets the top 5 content types and recommends 20 content items per type.

### Step 3: Schedule Notifications at Optimal Times
Notifications should be sent when the user is most active. Extract all active times, schedule notifications at those times, and add surprise slots during non-peak times to test engagement.

**Commands:**
```bash
# Schedule at all active times
jq -r ".activity[] | select(.type == \"active_time\") | .time" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq | xargs -I{} /app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time {} --message "Peak activity content bundle!"

# Add surprise slots
jq -r "12:15 14:30 16:45 19:00" | xargs -I{} /app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time {} --message "Surprise content digest!"
```
This ensures notifications during active periods and unexpected times to maintain engagement.

### Step 4: Reinforce Liked Content Recommendations
Recommend content similar to what the user has already liked, as this increases the chance of further interaction.

**Command:**
```bash
jq -r ".activity[] | select(.type == \"like\") | .content_id" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq | xargs -I{} /app/recommend_similar_content.sh --user user_x_1337 --content_id {}
```

### Step 5: Centralized Daily Digest
A daily digest at a prime time (e.g., 6 PM) can summarize new recommendations, encouraging the user to engage.

**Command:**
```bash
/app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time "18:00" --message "300+ new recommendations just for you!"
```

### Final Strategy
Combining all these steps ensures a multi-channel approach:
1. **Under-Engaged Content**: Target content the user viewed but didn't interact with.
2. **Content-Type Targeting**: Focus on types the user already engages with.
3. **Timely Notifications**: Maximize visibility during active times and surprise slots.
4. **Reinforced Recommendations**: Leverage liked content to drive further engagement.
5. **Digest Summary**: Encourage bulk engagement at a prime time.

This strategy diversifies triggers and increases relevance, aiming to sustain and boost DAET through sustained, varied interactions.

bash -c 'top_content_type=$(jq -r \".activity[] | select(.type == \"like\" or .type == \"share\") | .content_type\" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n1 | cut -d" " -f2); /app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time "12:00" --message "New $top_content_type content you'll love! Based on your favorites."'
bash -c 'untouched_types=$(jq -r \".activity[] | select(.type == \"view\" and not any(.following_siblings[0:100] | .type == \"like\" or .type == \"share\" or .type == \"comment\")) | .content_type\" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq | grep -vE "^(SELECTED_CONTENT_TYPES)"); for type in $untouched_types; do jq -r \".activity[] | select(.type == \"view\" and .content_type == \"$type\" and not any(.following_siblings[0:100] | .type == \"like\" or .type == \"share\" or .type == \"comment\")) | .content_id\" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n 30 | awk \'{print $2}\' | xargs -I{} /app/recommend_similar_content.sh --user user_x_1337 --content_id {}; done'
bash -c 'last_active=$(jq -r \".activity[] | select(.type == \"active_time\") | .time\" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | tail -n1); current_time=$(date +%H:%M); last_epoch=$(date -d "$last_active" +%s); current_epoch=$(date +%s); inactive=$((current_epoch - last_epoch)); if [ $inactive -gt 86400 ]; then /app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time "18:00" --message "We miss you! New content awaits!"; fi'
bash -c 'liked_content=$(jq -r \".activity[] | select(.type == \"like\") | .content_id\" /app/data/user_feed.json | sort | uniq -c | sort -nr | head -n 30 | cut -d" " -f2); for content_id in $liked_content; do for t in "09:00" "15:00" "21:00"; do /app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time "$t" --message "More like this: $content_id!"; done; done'
bash -c '/app/schedule_notification.sh --user user_x_1337 --time "11:00" --message "Midday surprise! Explore new categories you haven’t tried yet!"'
